On the problem of generalized measures: an impossibility result Thanks: This research was funded by the Austrian Science Fund (FWF) P35655 and P35588 , by the OeAD Ernst Mach grant - worldwide and the Austrian Science Fund (FWF) [10.55776/STA139] , by Università degli Studi of Udine (Italy) , by Progetto PRIN 2022 – Models, Sets and Classifications – Code no. 2022TECZJA CUP G53D23001890006, funded by the European Union – NextGenerationEU – PNRR M4 C2 I1.1 , by the Gruppo Nazionale per le Strutture Algebriche, Geometriche e le loro Applicazioni (GNSAGA) of the Istituto Nazionale di Alta Matematica (INdAM), and by the European Union Horizon 2020 research and innovation programme, under the Marie Sklodowska-Curie action 101210902 (TopAspOfGDST) . Claudio Agostini, Fernando Barrera, Vincenzo Dimonte

Abstract
. This paper investigates the problem of extending measure theory to non-separable structures, from generalized descriptive set theory to a broader class of spaces beyond this framework. While various notions, such as the ideal of measure zero sets, have been generalized, the question of whether a satisfactory notion of λ + \lambda^{+} -measure could be defined in generalized descriptive set theory has remained open. We introduce a broad class of λ + \lambda^{+} -measures as functions taking values in arbitrary positively totally ordered monoids equipped with an infinitary sum. This definition relies on minimal assumptions and captures most natural generalizations of measures to this context. We then prove that, under certain cardinal assumptions, no continuous λ + \lambda^{+} -measure of this kind exists on λ κ \mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}} , nor on any λ + \lambda^{+} -Borel space or T 0 T_{0} topological space of weight at most λ \lambda . We also show the optimality of these cardinal assumptions.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification

1.
Introduction

Generalized descriptive set theory has roots dating back to the second half of the last century (see, e.g.,
[
26
,
21
]
). Over the past decade, the field has expanded rapidly, with an increasing number of notions and results being extended from classical descriptive set theory to its generalized counterpart. For instance, the reducibility of Borel equivalence relations on the generalized Baire space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
, where
\kappa
is a regular uncountable cardinal such that
2^{<\kappa}=\kappa
, was studied in
[
11
]
, and classical results on Souslin quasi-orders were extended to this setting in
[
4
]
. More recently, the framework was further developed (see, e.g.,
[
6
,
14
,
2
]
) to include topological spaces of a more general form than
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-7.61604pt{\kappa}\kern 4.68175pt}}_{{\kern-13.82704pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.68175pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.95085pt{\kappa}\kern 2.82318pt}}_{{\kern-9.38727pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.82318pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\kappa^{{\kern-4.14423pt{\kappa}\kern 2.01656pt}}_{{\kern-8.58064pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.01656pt}}}
, showing that it is possible to introduce a suitable generalization of metrics in this context and that the resulting theory remains well-behaved. Other projects (see, e.g.,
[
8
,
3
,
1
]
) are aiming to further extend this theory to all cardinals
\lambda
satisfying
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda
, including singular cardinals.

Various regularity properties, such as the Baire property, the perfect set property, the Hurewicz dichotomy, and more, have been extended and studied in the generalized setting (see, e.g.,
[
19
,
25
,
18
]
). These results show that, at least consistently in certain cases, these properties can exhibit remarkably good behavior in the generalized context as well.

However, measure theory, a major topic of classical descriptive set theory with many applications to other areas of mathematics, has yet to find a proper analogue in the generalized setting.
There have been efforts to generalize concepts related to measures. For example,
[
12
]
and
[
5
]
proposed different generalizations of the ideal of null sets to the generalized Baire space. Yet, further progress has faced significant technical and conceptual obstacles. As a result, the question of whether a satisfactory notion of “measure” can be developed in this context has remained open.

In this paper, we settle this problem by showing that, under very mild assumptions and for a sufficiently large cardinal
\lambda
, it is impossible to define non-trivial generalized measures on a
\lambda^{+}
-Borel space. In particular, no such measure exists on the generalized Baire space.

We work in a slightly more general framework than the one usually adopted in generalized descriptive set theory (see Subsection
2.1
).
Our results apply to any space
X
of weight
\lambda
of the form
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-5.59818pt{\gamma}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-9.58469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-4.54817pt{\gamma}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-8.53468pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
, for some set
A
and ordinal
\gamma
, equipped with the bounded topology.
For spaces of this form, we argue that the appropriate notion of measure is that of a
\lambda^{+}
-additive measure (Proposition
2.3
).

In practice, we can reduce to an equivalent and more familiar setting.
We fix two arbitrary cardinals
\lambda
and
\kappa
, with
\kappa\leq\lambda
and
\kappa
regular, and work (until Section
8
) with subspaces of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
(Fact
2.4
and Corollary
2.5
).
This way, when
\kappa=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)
and
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda
, we recover the standard setup used in other papers in generalized descriptive set theory on a cardinal
\lambda
(see, e.g.,
[

]
).

However, we do not impose any cardinal assumption a priori.
This allows us to study the spaces
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.60278pt{\lambda}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.09445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.90279pt{\lambda}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.39445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
even when

or

, a case that is usually not covered in generalized descriptive set theory.
In contrast, we show that the assumption
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda
can always be made without loss of generality:
up to homeomorphism, every space of the form
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
is homeomorphic to a space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{{\kern-9.52486pt{\kappa}\kern 6.59056pt}}_{{\kern-15.73586pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.59056pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{{\kern-9.52486pt{\kappa}\kern 6.59056pt}}_{{\kern-15.73586pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.59056pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{{\kern-6.4485pt{\kappa}\kern 4.32083pt}}_{{\kern-10.88492pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.32083pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\lambda^{\prime}}^{{\kern-5.63184pt{\kappa}\kern 3.50417pt}}_{{\kern-10.06825pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.50417pt}}}
for some
\lambda^{\prime}
satisfying
(\lambda^{\prime})^{<\kappa}=\lambda^{\prime}
(Proposition
2.6
).
A similar statement holds for the generalized Cantor space (Proposition
2.8
).

One of the main challenges in obtaining a meaningful impossibility theorem is to work with a class of “generalized measures” broad enough to encompass all reasonable generalizations of measure one might consider. We achieve this by relaxing the assumptions of classical measures as much as possible, introducing an “umbrella definition” that captures most (if not all) natural generalizations of measure to subspaces of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Recall that a
(classical) measurable space
on a set
X
is a pair
(X,\mathfrak{M})
, where
X
is a set and
\mathfrak{M}
is a
\sigma
-algebra on
X
. If
X
is a topological space, it is further required that
\tau\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
, i.e., that
\mathfrak{M}
extends the
\sigma
-algebra of Borel sets of
X
. In classical descriptive set theory, one typically restricts attention to
Borel spaces
, that is, pairs
(X,\mathfrak{M})
where
X
is a second countable
T_{0}
topological space and
\mathfrak{M}
is the smallest
\sigma
-algebra generated by the topology of
X
.

These notions have straightforward generalizations to the uncountable setting.
We define a
\lambda^{+}
-measurable space
as a pair
(X,\mathfrak{M})
where
X
is a topological space and
\mathfrak{M}
is a
\lambda^{+}
-algebra containing all open subsets of
X
(that is, a family closed under unions and intersections of size at most
\lambda
, and under complements).
We call a pair
(X,\mathfrak{M})
a
\lambda^{+}
-Borel space
if
\mathfrak{M}
is a
\lambda^{+}
-algebra generated by a
T_{0}
topology of weight at most
\lambda
, or, equivalently, if
\mathfrak{M}
separates the points of
X
and is generated by a subfamily of size at most
\lambda
(see, e.g.,
[
1
, Proposition 3.24]
).

As our aim is to relax these assumptions as much as possible, we work with a larger class of structures that extends the classes of
\lambda^{+}
-measurable and
\lambda^{+}
-Borel spaces. In particular, we drop the requirement that
\mathfrak{M}
is closed under complements or intersections, and we do not even require that
\mathsf{Bor}(X)\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
.

Definition 1.1
.

A
weakly
\lambda^{+}
-measurable space
is a pair
(X,\mathfrak{M})
where
X
is a topological space and
\mathfrak{M}
is a family of subsets of
X
closed under unions of size
\leq\lambda
and containing all open subsets of
X
.

On a classical measurable space
(X,\mathfrak{M})
, one typically defines measures as functions on
\mathfrak{M}
taking values in
\mathbb{R}_{\infty}=[0,\infty)\cup{\infty}
. More precisely, a
(classical) measure

\mu
on a measurable space
(X,\mathfrak{M})
is a partial function
\mu:\powerset(X)\to\mathbb{R}_{\infty}

satisfying that
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\dom(\mu)
,
\mu(\emptyset)=0
, and

\mu(\bigcup_{i\in\omega}A_{i})=\sum_{i\in\omega}\mu(A_{i})

for any countable family
(A_{i})_{i<\omega}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of disjoint sets.
We call
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
a
(classical) measure space
. To avoid trivialities, it is often required that
\mu(X)>0
to ensure that
\mu
is not constantly
0
.

To extend this definition to the uncountable case, one needs an ordered structure equipped with an operation extendable to sequences of the required (infinite) length. In the classical case, this role is played by the positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{R}_{\infty}
together with the infinitary operation
\sum
.

While there have been attempts to generalize the real numbers to uncountable settings while preserving as many of the properties of
\mathbb{R}
as possible (see, e.g.,
[
7
,
14
,
27
]
), we follow a different approach. Since our aim is to establish an impossibility theorem as general as possible, we work with an arbitrary positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{S}=(S,0,+,\leq)
, rather than restricting ourselves to a specific structure. Similar generalizations, where
\mathbb{R}
is replaced by monoids, have been successful in related contexts, such as that of metrics (see, e.g.,
[
23
]
).

As a generalization of infinite sums, we allow any partial function
\Sum:\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-14.47752pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 4.41669pt}}_{{\kern-36.3669pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.41669pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-14.47752pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 4.41669pt}}_{{\kern-36.3669pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.41669pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-9.94029pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-25.57555pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-9.1625pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 1.94444pt}}_{{\kern-24.79776pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.94444pt}}}\to\mathbb{S}
that agrees with
+
on finite sequences in its domain and is
continuous
on sequences of limit length, that is, satisfying

\Sum(s)=\sup\{\Sum(s\restriction\alpha)\mid\alpha<\operatorname{lh}(s),\ s\restriction\alpha\in\dom(\Sum)\}

for all
s\in\dom(\Sum)
such that
\operatorname{lh}(s)
is a limit ordinal and
s\restriction\alpha\in\dom(\Sum)
for cofinally many
\alpha<\operatorname{lh}(s)
(see Definition
3.1
). We refer to such an operation as an
infinitary operation
or
infinitary sum
on
\mathbb{S}
.

Measures taking values in a monoid with a non-continuous function
\Sum
typically fail to satisfy most of the fundamental properties that make the classical theory interesting.
For this reason, the study of these objects is limited to Subsection
5.2
, while in the rest of the paper we restrict our attention to continuous infinitary sums.

This setup ensures a lot of freedom, as monoids include a much wider variety of examples than fields or groups, with much more varied behavior. For example, while a totally ordered group of uncountable degree can never be Dedekind complete, its Dedekind completion does exist within the category of monoids (Proposition
3.10
).
At the same time, monoids can be extremely pathological.
They may fail the cancellation law and may contain idempotent elements, i.e. elements
a\in\mathbb{S}
such that
a+a=a
.
Moreover, the algebraic operation may interact poorly with the order structure and even be discontinuous: it can happen that there exists a bound
c\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
such that
a+a\geq c
for every
a\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
.
Since we aim to prove our impossibility theorem in full generality, we deliberately allow arbitrary monoids, including such ill-behaved examples.

The generality of this setting, however, requires us to impose additional conditions on the measures. These can be seen as analogues of properties that, in the classical case, follow from the algebraic structure of
\mathbb{R}_{\infty}
and the properties of
\sigma
-algebras (see, e.g.,
[
24
, Theorem 1.19]
). We incorporate a few of these properties directly into our definition. As customary in the literature, we write
x\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\mu(x)
in place of
\{x\}\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\mu(\{x\})
.

Definition 1.2
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M})
be a weakly
\lambda^{+}
-measurable space, and let
\mathbb{S}
be a positively totally ordered monoid with an infinitary sum
\Sum
.
A
\lambda^{+}
-measure
on
(X,\mathfrak{M})
is a partial function
\mu:\powerset(X)\to(\mathbb{S},\Sum)
such that
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\dom(\mu)
and:

(M1)

\mu(\emptyset)=0
,

(M2)

\mu(X)>0
,

(M3)

A\subseteq B
implies
\mu(A)\leq\mu(B)
for all
A,B\in\mathfrak{M}
,

(M4)

for every family of disjoint sets
(A_{i})_{i<\gamma}\subseteq\mathfrak{M}
of order type
\gamma<\lambda^{+}
, we have

•

\langle\mu(A_{i})\mid i<\gamma\rangle\in\dom(\Sum)
,

•

\mu(\bigcup_{i\in\gamma}A_{i})=\Sum_{i\in\gamma}\mu(A_{i})
,

(M5)

\mu
is
point-regular
, i.e., for every
x\in X
such that
x\in\mathfrak{M}
and for every (open) local basis
\{A_{i}\mid i<\gamma\}
at
x
of order type
\gamma<\lambda^{+}
, we have

\mu(x)=\inf_{i<\gamma}\mu(A_{i}).

We call
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
a
weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
.
We say that
\mu
is
continuous
if for every
x\in X
we have
x\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\mu(x)=0
.

It turns out that even under these weak conditions, it is already impossible to obtain a continuous measure on (any subspace of) the generalized Baire space.

Theorem 1.3
.

Let
\gamma
be an ordinal, let
A
be a set, and let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-5.59818pt{\gamma}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-9.58469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-4.54817pt{\gamma}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-8.53468pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
be a space of weight
\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Then, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

In particular, if
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda
and
\lambda
is at least the cardinality of the continuum
\mathfrak{c}
, then there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
(Corollary
8.1
).

Working with a
\lambda^{+}
-measure and with a monoid of such a general form requires substantially different techniques from those used with classical measures on
\mathbb{R}_{\infty}
. Therefore, in Sections
2
–
4
, we analyze the basic properties of monoids, develop the definitions of measure and infinitary sum, and show how the problem can be reduced to a simpler setup.

In Section
5
, we prove the optimality of the assumptions in Theorem
1.3
.
In Subsection
5.1
, we analyze the case
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda<\mathfrak{c}
, which implies
\kappa=\omega
. We show that in this case there are continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure spaces
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.9042pt{\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.61424pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.9042pt{\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.61424pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.14804pt{\omega}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.94093pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.33138pt{\omega}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-9.12427pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
(Proposition
5.3
). However, these measures, which we call
\lambda
-Dirac, are “trivial” in the sense that they are concentrated only on a small part of the space, in analogy with Dirac measures in the classical setting.
In Subsection
5.2
we show that if one further relaxes the assumptions on the monoid and allows an infinitary function
\Sum
that is not continuous, then a continuous (non-
\lambda
-Dirac), weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
can again be obtained on
X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
even when
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
(Theorem
5.17
).
We argue, however, that measures obtained in this way are highly artificial, and that allowing a non-continuous function
\Sum
generally produces pathological objects (see, e.g., Proposition
5.13
); for this reason, we disregard this notion.

In Section
6
, we prove Theorem
1.3
. In Proposition
6.10
, we establish a more technical impossibility result, showing that, even when
\lambda<\mathfrak{c}
, every continuous measure can be decomposed into
\omega_{1}
-Dirac measures. This nicely complements Theorem
1.3
and the results of Subsection
5.1
, showing that every continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure is essentially obtained by starting from a countable measure on a separable subspace and then extending it to the whole space.
This rigidity phenomenon highlights the essential uniqueness of the classical countable case and reinforces the idea that the notion of measure fundamentally captures the separable situation.

In Section
7
, we study continuous weak
\lambda
-measure spaces
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
We show that, if the structure
\mathbb{S}
is allowed to range over all positively totally ordered monoids, such
\lambda
-measures always exist (Proposition
7.1
).
In contrast, if
\mathbb{S}
is fixed to be the unit interval
[0,1]
, the existence of such
\lambda
-measure spaces is equivalent to the (real-valued-)measurability of
\lambda
(Proposition
7.2
and Corollary
7.3
).

Finally, in Section
8
, we briefly discuss the consequences of our results.
In Corollaries
8.1
–
8.6
, we give a complete summary of the existence and non-existence of measures on the spaces
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
and
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.60278pt{\lambda}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.09445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.90279pt{\lambda}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.39445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
across all possible cardinal scenarios.
We then show that, for
\lambda^{+}
-measure spaces, all results follow as consequences of the corresponding results for weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure spaces.
This allows us to extend our conclusions to a broader class of spaces, including
\lambda^{+}
-Borel spaces and more general topological spaces (that is, where
X
is not necessarily a subset of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
), showing for example the following.

Corollary

(Corollary
8.10
)
.

Assume
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda>\omega
. Let
X
be a
T_{0}
topological space of weight
\leq\lambda
. Then, there is no continuous
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

Corollary

(Corollary
8.11
)
.

Assume
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda>\omega
. Let
(X,\mathfrak{M})
be a
\lambda^{+}
-Borel space. Then, there is no continuous
\lambda^{+}
-measure on it.

2.
Setup

We start by reviewing briefly the key notions we use throughout the paper.

2.1.
Topology and generalized descriptive set theory

We refer the reader to
[
9
]
and to
[
4
,
3
,
2
]
for the basic notions of, respectively, general topology and generalized descriptive set theory.

Recall that given a set
A
and an ordinal
\gamma
, we denote by
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-5.59818pt{\gamma}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-9.58469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-4.54817pt{\gamma}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-8.53468pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
the set of sequences of elements of

of length

. We also let
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[12.72676pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.72676pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.34798pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.53429pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-12.473pt{<\gamma}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-19.19023pt\mathchoice{\makebox[12.72676pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[12.72676pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.34798pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.53429pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}=\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\alpha}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.19876pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.13454pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.23895pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-9.25957pt{\alpha}\kern 2.625pt}}}
.
The bounded topology on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-5.59818pt{\gamma}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-9.58469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-4.54817pt{\gamma}\kern 2.625pt}}_{{\kern-8.53468pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}}
is generated by the sets (called
cones
)
\mathbf{N}_{s}(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.675pt}}_{{\kern-9.58469pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.625pt}}})=\{x\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\gamma}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}A^{{\kern-8.6619pt{\gamma}\kern 6.01392pt}}_{{\kern-14.243pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.20052pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.53687pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.81206pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern for
