2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}}\cup Y))\cup D_{1}
are such that
A_{0}\downarrow Y=D_{0}
and
A_{1}\downarrow Y=D_{1}
are disjoint, while at the same time we have
\mu(A_{0})=\mu(A_{1})=0
and
\mu(A_{0}\cup A_{1})=\mu(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-5.33972pt{\omega}\kern 3.05002pt}}_{{\kern-10.13261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.05002pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-4.46829pt{\omega}\kern 2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}})>0
(and
\mu(M)=\mu(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-5.33972pt{\omega}\kern 3.05002pt}}_{{\kern-10.13261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.05002pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-4.46829pt{\omega}\kern 2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}})>0
for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
such that
M\cap Y=(A_{0}\cup A_{1})\cap Y=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-5.33972pt{\omega}\kern 3.05002pt}}_{{\kern-10.13261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.05002pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-4.46829pt{\omega}\kern 2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}}
).
Therefore
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is not countable-induced.
∎

This is not a real obstacle, however, as it is clear that even the weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
from Proposition
5.10
is “essentially” countable-induced, in the sense that its
kernel
is.
More in general, we are going to show that if
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is
\lambda
-partitionable, then it is
\omega_{1}
-Dirac if and only if it is countable-induced.

Proposition 5.11
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a continuous
\lambda^{+}
-partitionable weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space. Then, the following are equivalent:

(a)

(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is countable-induced.

(b)

(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is
\omega_{1}
-Dirac.

Proof.

First, assume
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is countable-induced. By Proposition
5.7
there is a closed, separable
Y\subseteq X
and a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(Y,\mathfrak{N},\nu)
such that
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq\mathfrak{N}\uparrow X
, and
\mu\subseteq\nu\uparrow X
.
Then,
X\setminus Y
is open, thus measurable, and
\mu(X\setminus Y)=0
, therefore
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\subseteq Y
. By Remark
5.6
, we get that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is second countable as wanted.

Conversely, assume that
Y=\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is second countable, thus separable. Since
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is continuous, in particular
\mathfrak{M}
measures all points of
X
.
By Corollary
4.8
, we get that
X\setminus Y
has measure
0
, and thus
(Y,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow Y,\mu\downarrow Y)
is a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space by Corollary
4.24
. By Corollary
4.6
and Lemma
4.11
we have that
X\setminus Y
is essentially null. The inclusions
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq(\mathfrak{M}\downarrow Y)\uparrow X
and
\mu\subseteq(\mu\downarrow Y)\uparrow X
follow then by Lemma
4.19
.
∎

Since the kernel of a continuous,
\omega_{1}
-Dirac weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space is a continuous,
\omega_{1}
-Dirac minimal
\lambda^{+}
-measure space, and every minimal
\lambda^{+}
-measure space is
\lambda^{+}
-partitionable by Proposition
4.15
, we get the following.

Corollary 5.12
.

Every continuous,
\omega_{1}
-Dirac weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
has a countable-induced kernel
\ker(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

5.2.
A possibility theorem for monoids with non-continuous sum

In Theorem
1.3
we use an infinitary sum satisfying Axioms
(S1)
and
(S2)
. Clearly,
Axiom
(S1)
cannot be dropped.
In the following we show that we cannot drop Axiom
(S2)
either.

For the purpose of this subsection only and with a small abuse of notation, we call weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space even a triple
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
that satisfies all axioms and requirements of a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space, except that
\mu:\powerset(X)\to(\mathbb{S},\Sum)
takes values in a positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{S}
with a function
\Sum
that does not necessarily satisfy Axiom
(S2)
.
Notice that for such a measure we cannot apply the tools we developed in Section
4
.

First, without Axiom
(S2)
, there are subspaces of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
having a
\lambda^{+}
-measure.

Proposition 5.13
.

There is a positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{S}
with an infinitary function
\Sum
satisfying Axiom
(S1)
but not Axiom
(S2)
, and a subspace
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
for which there exists a continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on it.

Proof.

Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
be an infinite discrete subspace of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
. Consider the submonoid
\mathbb{S}=\{0,\infty\}
of
\mathbb{R}_{\infty}
.
Given
\bar{b}\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-14.47752pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 4.41669pt}}_{{\kern-36.3669pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.41669pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-14.47752pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 4.41669pt}}_{{\kern-36.3669pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.41669pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-9.94029pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 2.72223pt}}_{{\kern-25.57555pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.72223pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<{\sf On}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathbb{S}^{{\kern-9.1625pt{<{\sf On}}\kern 1.94444pt}}_{{\kern-24.79776pt\mathchoice{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[15.01947pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[9.94972pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[7.10695pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.94444pt}}}
, define
\Sum(\bar{b})=0
if
\bar{b}
is finite and constantly
0
, and
\Sum(\bar{b})=\infty
otherwise.
Define
\mu:\powerset(X)\to\mathbb{S}
by setting
\mu(M)=0
if
M
is finite, and
\mu(M)=\infty
otherwise. Then, it is clear that
\mu
satisfies Axioms
(M1)
-
(M3)
, and it satisfies trivially Axiom
(M5)
since every point is open. Also,
\mu
satisfies Axiom
(M4)
by the very definition of
\Sum
. Therefore,

(X,\powerset(X),\mu)
is a (weak)
\lambda^{+}
-measure space.
∎

The measure constructed in Proposition
5.13
is pathological in multiple ways, one of them being that it is
1
-Dirac: every point of
X
has an open neighborhood of measure
0
, hence
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)=\emptyset
. This observation alone might be regarded as sufficient motivation to exclude monoids with non-continuous functions
\Sum
. Nevertheless, for completeness, we also analyze what happens when we restrict our attention to non-Dirac measures, or to measures on
X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

First, the argument from Proposition
5.13
cannot be extended to the whole space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, since no
1
-Dirac measure exists on this space.

Proposition 5.14
.

Assume
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda
. Let
\mathbb{S}
be a positively totally ordered monoid with an infinitary function
\Sum
satisfying Axiom
(S1)
(but not necessarily Axiom
(S2)
).
Then there is no
1
-Dirac, continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Proof.

Assume, toward a contradiction, that
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is a continuous
1
-Dirac weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure.
In particular, the family
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu(V)=0\}
is a cover of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}
be a partition of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
refining
\mathcal{V}
, as given by Fact
2.1
.
Then,
\mu(P)=0
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, by Axiom
(M3)
, and
|\mathcal{P}|\leq\lambda
since we assumed
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
|\mathcal{P}|=\lambda
, for example by splitting one clopen cone of
\mathcal{P}
into its successors.

By Axioms
(M2)
and
(M4)
, we obtain

0<\mu(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}})=\mu\Bigl(\bigcup_{P\in\mathcal{P}}P\Bigr)=\Sum((0)_{i<\lambda}),

and hence
\Sum((0)_{i<\lambda})>0
.
On the other hand, if
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.70824pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.07797pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.89157pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.2613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
is such that
\mathbf{N}_{s}\in\mathcal{P}
, then by Axiom
(M3)
we have
\mu(\mathbf{N}_{s\conc\alpha})=0
for every
\alpha<\lambda
.
By Axiom
(M4)
, this yields

0=\mu(\mathbf{N}_{s})=\mu\Bigl(\bigcup_{\alpha<\lambda}\mathbf{N}_{s\conc\alpha}\Bigr)=\Sum((0)_{i<\lambda}),

a contradiction.
∎

Notice that the contrast between Propositions
5.13
and
5.14
also shows that the results we developed in Section
4
– like Lemma
4.20
– cannot be applied in general to a function
\Sum
that does not satisfy Axiom
(S2)
.

Restricting our attention to non-Dirac measures, the next result shows that when
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
, non-Dirac continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure spaces cannot exist, even without assuming that
\Sum
is continuous.

Proposition 5.15
.

Assume
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
. Let
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, and let
\mathbb{S}
be a
positively totally ordered monoid with an infinitary function
\Sum
satisfying Axiom
(S1)
(but not necessarily Axiom
(S2)
).
Then there is no non-
1
-Dirac, continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
X
.

Proof.

Assume by contradiction that
\mu
is a continuous non-
1
-Dirac measure on
X
, i.e., all points of
X
are measurable of measure zero, and
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\neq\emptyset
.
Let
x\in\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
.
Let
\delta=\min\{|A|\mid A\text{ open neighborhood of }x\}
.
Notice that for every open neighborhood
A
of
x
, we have that
|A|\leq|\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}|=\lambda
, by
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
. Also,
\mu(A)>0
, by
x\in\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
. Therefore,

\mu(A)=\Sum((0)_{i<|A|})>0

by Axiom
(M4)
.
In particular, this shows that
\Sum((0)_{i<\delta})>0
.
By the minimality of
\delta
, for every open neighborhood
B
of
x
we can find an open
B^{\prime}\subseteq B
such that
|B^{\prime}|=\delta
, and thus

\mu(B)\geq\mu(B^{\prime})=\Sum((0)_{i<\delta})>0

by Axiom
(M3)
.
Let
(A_{i})_{i<\kappa}
be a local basis of
x
of length
\kappa
(for example
A_{i}=\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction i}
for every
i<\kappa
).
By the previous argument, we get

\inf_{i<\kappa}\mu(A_{i})=\Sum((0)_{i<\delta})>0=\mu(x).

Since the hypothesis
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
implies
\kappa<\lambda
, this shows that
\mu
does not satisfy Axiom
(M5)
, contradiction.
∎

In particular, Propositions
5.14
and
5.15
together show that, when
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
at all.

Corollary 5.16
.

Assume
\lambda=\lambda^{\kappa}
. Let
\mathbb{S}
be a
positively totally ordered monoid with an infinitary function
\Sum
satisfying Axiom
(S1)
(but not necessarily Axiom
(S2)
).
Then there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Under the additional hypothesis
\lambda<\lambda^{\kappa}
(which always holds when
\kappa=\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)
, our main case of interest), using a non-continuous function
\Sum
instead makes it possible to obtain measures on the whole space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Theorem 5.17
.

Assume
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda<\lambda^{\kappa}
. There is a non-
\lambda
-Dirac, continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
for some positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{S}
with an infinitary function
\Sum
satisfying Axiom
(S1)
but not Axiom
(S2)
.

Proof.

Let
X=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
Let
\mathbb{L}=\kappa^{\ast}=\{\frac{1}{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\}
be
\kappa
with reverse order, i.e. the total order given by
\frac{1}{\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{\beta}
if
\alpha\geq\beta
. Then,
\mathbb{L}=(\mathbb{L},\max,\leq)
is a totally ordered semigroup.

Let
\{s_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda\}
be a well-ordering of

Consider
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}=\mathbb{L}\times\mathbb{N}^{\lambda}
with lexicographic order and point-wise sum (where
\mathbb{N}=(\omega,0,+,\leq)
represent as usual the positively totally ordered monoid of natural numbers), and let
\mathbb{S}=\mathbb{S}^{\prime}\cup\{e\}
be the monoid obtained by adding an identity
e
(and minimum of the order) to the semigroup
\mathbb{S}^{\prime}
. Then

\mathbb{S}=(\{\frac{1}{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa\},e,\ast,\leq)

is a positively totally ordered monoid. The operation
\ast
can be defined explicitly by

\left(\frac{1}{\alpha},(n_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon<\lambda}\right)\ast\left(\frac{1}{\beta},(m_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon<\lambda}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{\min(\alpha,\beta)},(n_{\epsilon}+m_{\epsilon})_{\epsilon<\lambda}\right)

and the order is the lexicographic one.
Notice that
\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\kappa
, as
(\frac{1}{\alpha},(0)_{\epsilon<\lambda})_{\alpha<\kappa}
is strictly decreasing and coinitial in
\mathbb{S}
.

Notice that
\Sum
is not continuous. For example, assume without loss of generality that
s_{i}=\langle i\rangle
for every
i<\omega
, and let
A_{\alpha}=\bigcup_{i<\alpha}\mathbf{N}_{\langle i\rangle}
for every
\alpha\leq\omega
. Then, we have
\mathcal{EX}(A_{n})=A_{n}
for every
i\leq n
(by equation (
5.1
)), while
\mathcal{EX}(A_{\omega})=X
. Therefore, we get

\Sum_{i<\omega}s_{i}=\left(\frac{1}{h(X)},\chi_{X}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{0},(1)_{i<\lambda}\right),

\sup_{n<\omega}\Sum_{i<n}s_{i}=\sup_{n<\omega}\left(\frac{1}{h\left(A_{n}\right)},\chi_{A_{n}}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{h\left(A_{\omega}\right)},\chi_{A_{\omega}}\right)=\left(\frac{1}{1},\chi_{A_{\omega}}\right).

We show now that there is a continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
where the measure takes values in
\mathbb{S}
with the (
non-continuous!
) sum
\Sum
we defined.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be the minimal
\lambda^{+}
-measurable structure on
X
measuring all points and open sets of
X
.
We define a measure
\mu:\mathfrak{M}\to\mathbb{S}
by setting
\mu(M)=e
for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
with
\mathrm{int}(M)=\emptyset
, and

\mu\left(M\right)=\left(\frac{1}{h\left(\mathcal{EX}\left(\mathrm{int}(M)\right)\right)},\chi_{\mathcal{EX}\left(\mathrm{int}(M)\right)}\right)

for every other
M\in\mathfrak{M}
. It follows by definition that
\mu(M)
is adequate for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
with
\mathrm{int}(M)\neq\emptyset
.

We claim
\mu
is as wanted.

Indeed, Axioms
(M1)
-
(M2)
follow by definition.

Axiom
(M3)
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{EX}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{EX}(V)
,
\chi_{U}\leq\chi_{V}
, and
h(U)\geq h(V)
whenever
U\subseteq V
.

In order to prove Axiom
(M4)
, notice first that given two open sets
O,U
, we have either
O\cap U=\emptyset
or
|O\cap U|=\lambda^{\kappa}>\lambda
. Thus if
O
and
U
differ from a set of size
\leq\lambda
, we have
\mathcal{EX}(O)=\mathcal{EX}(U)
.
In particular, this shows that

(5.5)

\mu(O\cup D)=\mu(O)=\mu(O)\ast e=\mu(O)\ast\mu(D)

for every open
O
and every set
D
of size
\leq\lambda
disjoint from
O
.

Secondly, notice that

(5.6)

\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha})=\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha}))

for every family of open sets
\{O_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\gamma\}
.
The inclusion
\subseteq
is clear.
For the other inclusion
\supseteq
, notice that
\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha})\supseteq\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha})
, thus

\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha})=\mathcal{EX}(\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha}))\supseteq\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}\left(O_{\alpha}\right)),

as wanted.

Now given a family of disjoint measurable sets
(A_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\gamma}
of length
\gamma<\lambda^{+}
, let
O_{\alpha}=\mathrm{int}(A_{\alpha})
, let
O=\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha}
and
M=\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}A_{\alpha}
. By definition of
\mathfrak{M}
, we have
|M\setminus O|\leq\lambda
, thus
\mu(M)=\mu(O)
by equation (
5.5
).
Since the
A_{\alpha}
are disjoint and
\mu(A_{\alpha})
is adequate for every
\alpha<\gamma
, it follows from equation (
5.3
) that
(\mu(A_{\alpha}))_{\alpha<\gamma}
is good.
Therefore, applying the definition of
\Sum
if
\gamma
is infinite, or equation (
5.4
) if
\gamma
is finite, and using equations (
5.6
) and (
5.3
), we get

\displaystyle\mu(M)

\displaystyle=\mu(O)=

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}(O))},\chi_{\mathcal{EX}(O)}\right)=

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha}))},\chi_{\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}O_{\alpha})}\right)=

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha})))},\chi_{\mathcal{EX}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha}))}\right)=

\displaystyle=\Sum_{\alpha<\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha}))},\chi_{\mathcal{EX}(O_{\alpha})}\right)=

\displaystyle=\Sum_{\alpha<\gamma}\mu(A_{\alpha})

which proves Axiom
(M4)
.

For Axiom
(M5)
, it is enough to notice that for every
x\in X
, we have that
(\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}))_{\alpha<\kappa}=(\frac{1}{\alpha},\chi_{\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}})_{\alpha<\kappa}
is coinitial in
\mathbb{S}^{+}
.

Finally, notice that
\mu(O)\neq e
for every non-empty, open set
O\in\mathfrak{M}
, thus
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
. Since
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
has weight
\lambda
, we get that
\mu
is not
\lambda
-Dirac, as wanted.
∎

In a certain sense, this result points in the opposite direction of Theorem
1.3
, apparently suggesting that some analogues of measures could be defined in this setting.
However, Theorem
5.17
should be compared more closely to Proposition
7.1
than to Theorem
1.3
. The similarity between the proofs of Theorem
5.17
and Proposition
7.1
shows that the measure obtained in Theorem
5.17
is essentially a
\delta
-measure
4
4

4

In the specific case of the measure
\mu
constructed in the proof of Theorem
5.17
, an
\omega
-measure.
for some
\delta<\lambda^{+}
, which is forced to behave like a
\lambda^{+}
-measure by making it “jump” when needed through the non-continuity of
\Sum
.
For this reason, it should not be regarded as a natural generalization of classical measures.

More in general, we observe that without continuity of the sum, we can hardly expect a measure to satisfy any reasonable form of regularity.

6.
On the non-existence of continuous
\lambda^{+}
-measures

In the following section we are going to prove the Impossibility Theorem (Theorem
1.3
), the main result of our paper.

We are going to use multiple times the following easy corollary of Corollary
4.8
, so we state it explicitly to avoid repetitions.

Corollary 6.1
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space and let
U\subseteq X
be an open set in
X
such that
\mu(U)>0
. Then, for every family of open sets
\mathcal{V}
of
X
of measure zero there is
x\in U\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
4.8
we have that
\mu(\bigcup\mathcal{V})=0
, thus
U\nsubseteq\bigcup\mathcal{V}
by Axiom
(M3)
.
∎

We split the proof in multiple cases, depending on the algebraic structure of
\mathbb{S}
and on the value of
\kappa
.

6.1.
Measures in monoids of wrong degree

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume
\Deg(\mathbb{S})\neq\kappa
. Then, for every weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
and for every open set
U
of positive measure
\mu(U)>0
there is
x\in U
such that either
x
is not measurable or
\mu(x)>0
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu(V)=0\}
be the set of open sets of
X
of measure
0
.
By Corollary
6.1
, there is
x\in U\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
. By definition of
\mathcal{V}
, we get
\mu(A)>0
for every
A\in\tau
such that
x\in A
.
In particular, for every
\alpha<\beta<\kappa
we have
0<\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\beta}(X))\leq\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}(X))
by Axiom
(M3)
, thus the sequence
\langle\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}(X))\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is decreasing in
\mathbb{S}^{+}
.
Now, if
x
is measurable of measure
0
, this also implies that
\langle\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}(X))\mid\alpha<\kappa\rangle
is coinitial in
\mathbb{S}^{+}
by Axiom
(M5)
, hence
\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\kappa
.
∎

6.2.
Measures in non-
0
-continuous monoids

Recall that a positively totally ordered monoid
\mathbb{S}
is not
0
-continuous if and only if it contains an element
c
such that
b+b\geq c
for all
b\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
.

Lemma 6.3
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space. Let
U\subseteq X
be an open subset of
X
of positive measure
\mu(U)>0
whose value satisfies
b+b\geq\mu(U)
for every
b\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
.
Then, one of the following holds:

•

U
contains a point
x\in U
that is not measurable,

•

U
contains a measurable point
x\in U
of measure
\mu(x)=\mu(U)
,

•

U
contains two measurable points
x,y\in U
such that
\mu(x)+\mu(y)=\mu(U)
.

Proof.

If
U
contains a non-measurable point we are done, so assume instead that all points of
U
are measurable.
Also, if there is
x\in U
such that
\mu(U)=\mu(O)
for every open neighborhood
O
of
x
contained in
U
,
then we get
\mu(x)=\mu(U)
, by Axiom
(M5)
, and we are done. So let
c=\mu(U)
and suppose that every
x\in U
has an open neighborhood
O
of measure
\mu(O)<c
.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu(V)=0\}
be the set of open sets of
X
of measure
0
. Then,
U\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
is non-empty, by Corollary
6.1
.
Let
x_{1}\in U\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
.
Then,
\mu(x_{1})<c
, by Axiom
(M3)
and since
\mu(O)<c
for some neighborhood
O
of
x_{1}
.
This also implies that
\mu(U\setminus\{x_{1}\})>0
, since otherwise

\mu(U)=\mu(x_{1})+\mu(U\setminus\{x_{1}\})=\mu(x_{1})+0=\mu(x_{1})<c=\mu(U),

contradiction.
Thus, there is
x_{2}\in(U\setminus\{x_{1}\})\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
, by Corollary
6.1
.
Let
N_{1},N_{2}\subseteq U
be disjoint cones such that
x_{i}\in N_{i}
and
0<\mu(N_{i})<c
for each
i\in\{1,2\}
.

Fix
i\in\{1,2\}
. Notice that

\mu(V)=0
for every clopen
V\subseteq N_{i}\setminus\{x_{i}\}
, for otherwise both
\mu(V)\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
and
\mu(N_{i}\setminus V)\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
, and thus
\mu(N_{i})=\mu(V)+\mu(N_{i}\setminus V)\geq c>\mu(N_{i})
by assumption on
c
, contradiction.
Then,
\mu(N_{i})=\mu(O)+\mu(N_{i}\setminus O)=\mu(O)
for every clopen neighborhood
O
of
x_{i}
contained in
N_{i}
, and thus
\mu(x_{i})=\mu(N_{i})
, as before.

Then, by Axiom
(M3)
and by the assumption on
c
, we get

\mu(U)=c\leq\mu(N_{1})+\mu(N_{2})=\mu(N_{1}\cup N_{2})\leq\mu(U).

and thus
\mu(U)=\mu(N_{1})+\mu(N_{2})=\mu(x_{1})+\mu(x_{2})
as wanted.
∎

Corollary 6.4
.

Assume
\mathbb{S}
is not
0
-continuous. Then, for every weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
and for every open set
U
of positive measure
\mu(U)>0
there is
x\in U
such that either
x
is not measurable or
\mu(x)>0
.

Proof.

Let
c
be such that
b+b\geq c
for every
b\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
, that is, a witness of the non-
0
-continuity of
\mathbb{S}
.
Assume by contradiction that every point of
U
is measurable of measure
0
.
Then,
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid V\subseteq U,\mu(V)\leq c\}
is a cover of
U
, by Axiom
(M5)
.
Let also
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu(V)=0\}
be the set of open sets of
X
of measure
0
, then we can find
U^{\prime}\in\mathcal{V}\setminus\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
, by Corollary
6.1
.
The result then follows from Lemma
6.3
applied to
U^{\prime}
.
∎

6.3.
Measures in non-Archimedean monoids

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space.
Assume
U\in\tau
and
a,b\in\mathbb{S}
are such that
a\ll b<\mu(U)
.
Then, there is
x\in U
such that either
x
is not measurable or it has measure
\mu(x)\geq a
.

Proof.

Assume that
x\in\mathfrak{M}
for every
x\in U
, as otherwise we are done.
Define
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu(V)\leq a\}
.
Suppose by contradiction that
\mathcal{V}
covers
U
.
By Fact
2.1
we can find a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
U
refining
\mathcal{V}
. By Axioms
(M3)
and
(M4)
and by Proposition
3.4
(b)
, it follows that

\mu(U)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mu(P)\leq\sum_{i<\lambda}a\leq b<\mu(U),

contradiction.
Thus
\mathcal{V}
cannot cover
U
. Let
x\in U\setminus\bigcup\mathcal{V}
. This implies
\mu(A)>a
for every
A\in\tau
such that
x\in A
. Therefore, we have
\mu(x)=\inf\{A\in\tau\mid x\in A\}\geq a
by Axiom
(M5)
, as wanted.
∎

Notice that if
\bar{a}=\langle a_{i}\mid i<\delta\rangle
is a nowhere Archimedean decreasing sequence of limit length that is coinitial in
\mathbb{S}^{+}
, then we have that for every
c>0
there are
i,j\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
such that
0<a_{i}\ll a_{j}<c
. Thus, we get the following.

Corollary 6.6
.

Assume
\mathbb{S}^{+}
has infinite degree and contains a nowhere Archimedean decreasing coinitial sequence. Then, for every weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
and for every open set
U
of positive measure
\mu(U)>0
there is
x\in U
such that either
x
is not measurable or
\mu(x)>0
.

6.4.
Dirac-like measures above the continuum

Lemma 6.7
.

Assume
\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be an
\omega_{1}
-Dirac, weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space.
Then, for every open set
U
of positive measure
\mu(U)>0
there is
x\in U
such that either
x
is not measurable or
\mu(x)>0
.

Proof.

Assume that
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is
\omega_{1}
-Dirac, and thus
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is second countable.
Recall that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is closed, thus
U\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is measurable.
By Corollary
4.8
, we get that
\mu(X\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu))=0
, thus
\mu(U\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu))=0
as well by Axiom
(M3)
.
By Remark
5.6
, we get that
|\operatorname{supp}(\mu)|\leq|\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-8.1989pt{\omega}\kern 5.03777pt}}_{{\kern-14.90894pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 5.03777pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-5.33972pt{\omega}\kern 3.05002pt}}_{{\kern-10.13261pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.05002pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\omega^{{\kern-4.46829pt{\omega}\kern 2.17859pt}}_{{\kern-9.26118pt\mathchoice{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[5.03777pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[3.05002pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.17859pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.17859pt}}}|\leq\mathfrak{c}\leq\lambda
.
If all points of
U
are measurable of measure
0
, then we have that
\mathcal{A}=\{U\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\}\cup\{\{x\}\mid x\in U\cap\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\}
is a cover of
U
of size
|\mathcal{A}|\leq\lambda
made of disjoint sets of measure
0
.
By Axiom
(M4)
, we would get

\mu(U)=\mu(U\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu))+\sum_{x\in U\cap\operatorname{supp}(\mu)}\mu(x)=0,

contradiction.
∎

6.5.
Proof of the Impossibility Theorem

We prove the Impossibility Theorem
1.3
in a stronger, although more technical, form.

First, the results obtained so far already allow us to conclude that when
\kappa
is uncountable, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on any subspace
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
.

Proposition 6.8
.

Assume
\kappa>\omega
.
Then, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

Proof.

If
\mathbb{S}
is not
0
-continuous or has finite degree, the conclusion follows from Corollary
6.4
.
If
\Deg(\mathbb{S})\neq\kappa
, the conclusion follows from Lemma
6.2
.
Thus, we may assume that
\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\kappa>\omega
and that
\mathbb{S}
is
0
-continuous.
Then, by Lemma
2.12

\mathbb{S}
contains a coinitial nowhere Archimedean sequence, and hence by Corollary
6.6
we are done.
∎

When
\kappa=\omega
, continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure spaces may exist.
The following results show, however, that in this case the measure is always trivial, in the sense that it is a sum of
\omega_{1}
-Dirac measures.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space.
Then, there is a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
X
such that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap P
is second countable for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
6.4
, we get that
\mathbb{S}
is
0
-continuous and of infinite degree. By Corollary
6.6
,
\mathbb{S}
contains no coinitial nowhere Archimedean sequence.
Thus, by Lemma
2.12
, we have that
\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\omega
and
\mathbb{S}
is initially Archimedean.
By Lemma
6.2
, we have that
\kappa=\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\omega
.

Say that
\mu
is
essentially countable on
V
if for every family
\mathcal{A}
of disjoint open subsets of
V
there are at most countably many elements of
\mathcal{A}
of positive measure.

Claim 6.9.1
.

Let
\kappa=\omega
. Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space, and let
V\subseteq X
be open.
Suppose that
\mu
is essentially countable on
V
.
Then,
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap V
is second countable.

Proof.

Let
T=\{s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.71532pt{<\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-29.53197pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.71532pt{<\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-29.53197pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.87027pt{<\omega}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.59648pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0536pt{<\omega}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.77982pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\mid\mu(\mathbf{N}_{s})>0,\mathbf{N}_{s}\subseteq V\}
.
By assumption, each level
