\mathrm{Lev}_{n}(T)=\{s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.79533pt{n}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.26584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.79533pt{n}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.26584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.07027pt{n}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.69205pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.2536pt{n}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.87538pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\mid\mu(\mathbf{N}_{s})>0,\mathbf{N}_{s}\cap X\subseteq V\}
must be countable, since
\{\mathbf{N}_{s}\mid s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.79533pt{n}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.26584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.79533pt{n}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-14.26584pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-5.07027pt{n}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.69205pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{n}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.2536pt{n}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.87538pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.94333pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.94115pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.10081pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\}
is a family of disjoint open sets.
This implies that
T=\bigcup_{n\in\omega}\mathrm{Lev}_{n}(T)
is countable as well.
Since
\{\mathbf{N}_{s}\mid s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.71532pt{<\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-29.53197pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.71532pt{<\omega}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-29.53197pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.87027pt{<\omega}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.59648pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\omega}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.0536pt{<\omega}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.77982pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.56401pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.86113pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.90082pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\}
is a basis for the topology of
X
, and
V
is open, we have that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap V=[T]
, and that
\{\mathbf{N}_{s}\mid s\in T\}
is a basis for
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
. Therefore,
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is second countable as wanted.
∎

Let
\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\mu\text{ is essentially countable on }V\}
. Assume first that

\mathcal{V}
does not cover
X
.
Let
a,b\in\mathbb{S}^{+}
be such that the interval
[0,b]
is Archimedean and
0<a<b
.
Let
\mathcal{A}=\{A\in\tau\mid\mu(A)<a\}
, then we have that
\mathcal{A}
is a cover of
X
, by Axiom
(M5)
and since
\mu(x)=0
for every
x\in X
.
Therefore, there is
A\in\mathcal{A}\setminus\mathcal{V}
. Since
\mu
is not essentially countable on
A
, there is an uncountable family
\mathcal{P}
of disjoint clopen subsets of
A
such that
0<\mu(P)
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
. By Axiom
(M3)
, we also have
\mu(P)\leq\mu(A)<a
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
.
By the pigeonhole principle, since
\Deg(\mathbb{S})=\omega
, there is
c\in(0,a)
such that
\mu(P)>c
for uncountably many
P\in\mathcal{P}
.
Since
[0,b]
is Archimedean and
c\in[0,b]
, we can find
n\in\omega
such that
n\cdot c\geq b
.
But then,

\mu(A)\geq\mu(\bigcup\mathcal{P})=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}}\mu(P)\geq n\cdot c\geq b>a,

by Axioms
(M3)
and
(M4)
, contradicting that
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

Thus
\mathcal{V}
does cover
X
. By Fact
2.1
, we can find a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
X
refining
\mathcal{V}
.
It is easy to check that
\mu
is still essentially countable on every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, since
\mathcal{P}
refines
\mathcal{V}
.
By Claim
6.9.1
, for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
we have that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap P
is second countable, as wanted.
∎

Proposition 6.10
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space such that
\mathfrak{M}
is closed under finite intersections.

Then, there is a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
X
such that
(P,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow P,\mu\downarrow P)
is an
\omega_{1}
-Dirac, continuous, weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
and
\mu
is the sum of
(\mu\downarrow P)_{P\in\mathcal{P}}
, i.e., for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
we have

\mu(M)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}}(\mu\downarrow P)(M\cap P).

Proof.

By Lemma
6.9
, there is a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
of
X
such that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap P
is second countable for every
P\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
. Since we assumed that
X
has weight at most
\lambda
, we get that
|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|\leq\lambda
.
Therefore, since
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
covers
X
and is made of disjoint sets, there is
A\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
such that
\mu(A)>0
, by Axioms
(M2)
and
(M4)
.
Let

\mathcal{P}=\{P\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\setminus\{A\}\mid\mu(P)>0\}\cup\{A\cup\bigcup\{P\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mid\mu(P)=0\}\}.

This way,
\mu(P)>0
for every
P\in P
.
Since
|\mathcal{P}^{\prime}|\leq\lambda
, by Axiom
(M4)
we get that
\mu(\bigcup\{P\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mid\mu(P)=0\})=0
, and thus by Axiom
(M3)
we have

\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap(A\cup\bigcup\{P\in\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\mid\mu(P)=0\})=\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap A.

This shows that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap P
is still second countable for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
.
By Lemma
4.21
, we have that

(P,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow P,\mu\downarrow P)
is a (continuous) weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
. Since
P
is open, we get that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu\downarrow P)=\operatorname{supp}(\mu)\cap P
, and thus
(P,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow P,\mu\downarrow P)
is
\omega_{1}
-Dirac as well.

Finally, from the fact that
\mathfrak{M}
is closed under finite intersections we get that
M\cap P\in\mathfrak{M}
for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
and
(\mu\downarrow P)(M\cap P)=\mu(M\cap P)
, and thus from Axiom
(M4)
we get

\mu(M)=\sum_{P\in\mathcal{P}}(\mu\downarrow P)(M\cap P),

for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
, i.e.
\mu
is the sum of
(\mu\downarrow P)_{P\in\mathcal{P}}
, as wanted.
∎

In particular, Proposition
6.10
applies to every continuous minimal
\lambda^{+}
-measure space, thanks to Proposition
4.14
.

Corollary 6.11
.

Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a continuous, minimal
\lambda^{+}
-measure space. Then, there is a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
X
such that
(P,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow P,\mu\downarrow P)
is an
\omega_{1}
-Dirac, continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
, and
\mu
is the sum of the measures
(\mu\downarrow P)_{P\in\mathcal{P}}
.

In particular, this holds for the kernel
\ker(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
of any continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

Corollaries
2.5
and
6.11
and Lemma
6.7
together lead immediately to Theorem
1.3
, as wanted.

From Theorem
1.3
, we can also obtain the following statement.

Corollary 6.12
.

Assume
\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space. Then, there is a partition
X=C\cup O
such that
O
is open,
\mu(O)=0
, and
x\in\mathfrak{M}
for every
x\in O
, while
C
is closed and contains a dense subset
D\subseteq C
such that for every
x\in D
, either
x
is not measurable or
\mu(x)>0
.

Proof.

Let

\mathcal{V}=\{V\in\tau\mid\text{ There exists }x\in V,x\notin\mathfrak{M}\lor\mu(x)>0\},

\mathcal{U}=\{U\in\tau\setminus\mathcal{V}\mid\mu(U)=0\}.

Suppose by contradiction there is
Y\in\tau\setminus(\mathcal{U}\cup\mathcal{V})
: then,
\mu(Y)>0
and for every
x\in Y
, we have
x\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\mu(x)=0
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is minimal, up to passing to its kernel.
By Corollary
4.25
, we have that
(Y,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow Y,\mu\downarrow Y)
is a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space, and
\mu\downarrow Y
is continuous since
Y\notin\mathcal{V}
, contradicting Theorem
1.3
.

Therefore, we must have that
\mathcal{U}\cup\mathcal{V}
is a cover of
X
. Let
O=\bigcup\mathcal{U}
: then,
\mu(O)=0
, by Corollary
4.8
, and furthermore
x\in\mathfrak{M}
and
\mu(x)=0
for every
x\in O
, since by definition
U\notin\mathcal{V}
for every
U\in\mathcal{U}
.
Let
C=X\setminus O
.
Now for every
V\in\tau
such that
V\cap C\neq\emptyset
, we have that
V\in\mathcal{V}
, since
\mathcal{U}\cup\mathcal{V}
covers
X
, and thus we may chose
x_{V}\in V
such that
x\notin\mathfrak{M}
or
\mu(x)>0
. Then,
D=\{x_{V}\mid V\in\tau,V\cap C\neq\emptyset\}
is as wanted.
∎

7.
On the existence of
\lambda
-measures and measurable cardinals

In this section, we briefly investigate
\lambda
-measures on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
and their relationship with the measurability of
\lambda
.

The next result shows that when
\lambda
is regular,
\lambda
-measures do exist on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
This will allow us to complete the picture concerning the existence and non-existence of measures on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
in Section
8
(see Corollary
8.5
).

The regularity of
\lambda
is, in a certain sense, a necessary requirement: if
\lambda
is singular, then every weak
\lambda
-measure space is also a weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space. In particular, in this case the existence of a
\lambda
-measure is tied to the existence of a
\lambda^{+}
-measure, which we already addressed earlier.
For a singular cardinal
\lambda
, we can still show the existence of
\delta
-measures on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
for every regular cardinal
\delta\leq\lambda
.

Proposition 7.1
.

Let
\delta
be a regular cardinal such that
\delta\leq\lambda
. Then there is a continuous weak
\delta
-measure space
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
with
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)=\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

The proof closely follows that of Theorem
5.17
and can be viewed as a more direct version of it.

Proof.

Let
\mathbb{L}=(\kappa+1)^{\ast}=\{\frac{1}{\alpha}\mid\alpha\in\kappa+1\}
be
\kappa+1
with reverse order, i.e. the total order given by
\frac{1}{\alpha}\leq\frac{1}{\beta}
if
\alpha\geq\beta
. Then,
\mathbb{L}=(\mathbb{L},\frac{1}{\kappa},\max,\leq)
is a positively totally ordered monoid with identity
\frac{1}{\kappa}
. We consider the infinitary operation
\sum=\sup(=\max)
on it, which is total since
\mathbb{L}
is Dedekind-complete.

For every open set
O
, define

\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}\left(O\right)=\bigcup\{\mathbf{N}_{s}\mid s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.70824pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.07797pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.89157pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.2613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},|\{\alpha<\lambda\mid\mathbf{N}_{s\conc\alpha}\cap O\neq\emptyset\}|\geq\delta\}

to be the
\delta
-expansion
of
O
.
Then, we get
\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O))=\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O)\supseteq O
, and
\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathbf{N}_{s})=\mathbf{N}_{s}
for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.70824pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.07797pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.89157pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.2613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
Also, by construction we have

(7.1)

\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\bigcup_{i<\gamma}O_{i})=\bigcup_{i<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O_{i})

for every family of open sets
\{O_{i}\mid i<\gamma\}
of size
\gamma<\delta
.

Define

h\left(O\right)=\min\{\beta<\kappa\mid\mathbf{N}_{s}\subseteq O\text{ for some }s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.62575pt{\beta}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.72318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.62575pt{\beta}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.72318pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.94914pt{\beta}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.30444pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\beta}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.13248pt{\beta}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.48778pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.53441pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.77156pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.97969pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}\}.

to be the
height
of
O
.
We claim that

(7.2)

h(\bigcup_{i<\gamma}O_{i})=\min_{i<\gamma}h(O_{i})

for every family of open sets
\{O_{i}\mid i<\gamma\}
such that
\gamma<\delta
and
O_{i}=\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O_{i})
for every
i<\gamma
.
Indeed, let
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.70824pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.07797pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.89157pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.2613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
be such that
\mathbf{N}_{s}\subseteq\bigcup_{i<\gamma}O_{i}
.
Then,

|\{\alpha<\lambda\mid\mathbf{N}_{s\conc\alpha}\cap\bigcup_{i<\gamma}O_{i}\neq\emptyset\}|\geq\delta,

and
since
\delta=\operatorname{cof}(\delta)>\gamma
, there is
i<\gamma
such that

|\{\alpha<\lambda\mid\mathbf{N}_{s\conc\alpha}\cap O_{i}\neq\emptyset\}|\geq\delta.

Thus,
\mathbf{N}_{s}\subseteq\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O_{i})=O_{i}
, as wanted.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be the minimal
\delta
-measurable structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
measuring all points of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
Define
\mu(M)=\frac{1}{\kappa}
for every
M\in\mathfrak{M}
with empty interior, while for every other
M\in\mathfrak{M}
we define

\mu(M)=\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathrm{int}(M)))}.

Then, Axioms
(M1)
-
(M2)
follow by definition.

Axiom
(M3)
follows from the fact that
\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(U)\subseteq\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(V)
and
h(U)\geq h(V)
whenever
U\subseteq V
.

To prove Axiom
(M4)
, notice first that given two open sets
O,U
, we have either
O\cap U=\emptyset
or
|O\cap U|\geq\lambda\geq\delta
.
Thus, if the symmetric difference between
O
and
U
has size
|O\triangle U|<\delta
, we have
O\cap\mathbf{N}_{s}\neq\emptyset
if and only if
U\cap\mathbf{N}_{s}\neq\emptyset
for every
s\in\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-11.48848pt{<\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-28.80609pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.70824pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-20.07797pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{<\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-6.89157pt{<\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-19.2613pt\mathchoice{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[13.208pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[6.6343pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[4.73878pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
. This shows that
|O\triangle U|<\delta
implies
\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(O)=\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(U)
.
Since for every family of measurable sets
\{M_{i}\mid i<\gamma\}
of size
\gamma<\delta
we have

|\mathrm{int}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}M_{i})\setminus\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathrm{int}(M_{i})|\leq|\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}M_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathrm{int}(M_{i})|\leq|\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}(M_{i}\setminus\mathrm{int}(M_{i}))|<\delta,

by the definition of minimal
\delta
-measurable structure and the regularity of
\delta
, then

(7.3)

\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathrm{int}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}M_{i}))=\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathrm{int}(M_{i})).

Now given a family of disjoint measurable sets
(A_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\gamma}
of length
\gamma<\delta
, by combining equations (
7.1
), (
7.2
), and (
7.3
) we obtain

\displaystyle\mu(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}A_{i})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathrm{int}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}A_{i})))}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathrm{int}(A_{i})))}=

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{h(\bigcup_{\alpha<\gamma}\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathrm{int}(A_{i})))}=

\displaystyle=\sup_{\alpha<\gamma}\frac{1}{h(\mathcal{EX}_{\delta}(\mathrm{int}(A_{i})))}=

\displaystyle=\sum_{\alpha<\gamma}\mu(A_{i}).

Finally, for Axiom
(M5)
it is enough to notice that for every
x\in X
, we have that
(\mu(\mathbf{N}_{x\restriction\alpha}))_{\alpha<\kappa}=(\frac{1}{\alpha})_{\alpha<\kappa}
is coinitial in
\mathbb{S}^{+}
.
∎

Notably, the measure obtained in Proposition
7.1
takes values in a non-Archimedean monoid.
Requiring a measure that takes values in
[0,1]
, instead, leads to a characterization of (real-valued) measurable cardinals.

Proposition 7.2
.

The following are equivalent:

(1)

There is a non-countable-induced continuous weak
\lambda
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
for some
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
such that
\mu
takes values in
[0,1]
.

(2)

For every non-
\lambda
-Lindelöf space
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
there is
\mu:\powerset(Y)\to[0,1]
such that
(X,\powerset(X),\mu)
is a non-countable-induced continuous weak
\lambda
-measure space.

(3)

\lambda
is real-valued measurable.

Proof.

(1)
\Rightarrow
(3)
. Assume first that
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is a non-countable-induced, continuous, weak
\lambda
-measure space such that
\mu
takes values in
[0,1]
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume
\mathfrak{M}
is the minimal
\lambda
-measurable structure measuring all points of
X
.

The fact that
\mu(X)=1
and
[0,1]
is Archimedean together imply that every family of disjoint open sets of positive measure is countable.
This and Proposition
2.3
together, or the same argument of Claim
6.9.1
, imply that
\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
is second countable.

Let
Y=X\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
, we claim
\mu(Y)>0
. Suppose not: then, by Corollary
4.25
we have that
(\operatorname{supp}(\mu),\mathfrak{M}\downarrow\operatorname{supp}(\mu),\mu\downarrow\operatorname{supp}(\mu))
is a weak
\lambda
-measure space, and by Proposition
4.15
, Corollary
4.6
, and Lemmata
4.11
and
4.19
, we get that
\mathfrak{M}\subseteq(\mathfrak{M}\downarrow\operatorname{supp}(\mu))\uparrow X
and
\mu\subseteq(\mu\downarrow\operatorname{supp}(\mu))\uparrow X
. This shows that
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is countable-induced, contradiction.

Therefore,
\mu(Y)>0
. Since
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
is minimal and continuous, by Proposition
4.14
and Lemma
4.21
we have that
(Y,\mathfrak{M}\downarrow Y,\mu\downarrow Y)
is a
\lambda
-measure on
Y
taking values in
[0,1]
. Since
Y=X\setminus\operatorname{supp}(\mu)
, the family of open sets of measure zero covers
Y
, and by Fact
2.1
there is a clopen partition
\mathcal{P}
of
Y
such that
\mu(P)=0
for every
P\in\mathcal{P}
.
Notice that
|\mathcal{P}|\leq\lambda
, since
Y\subseteq X
has weight
\lambda
, and
|\mathcal{P}|=\lambda
since
\mu
is a
\lambda
-measure and
\mu(Y)=\mu(\bigcup\mathcal{P})>0
.
Let
\{P_{i}\mid i<\lambda\}
be an enumeration of
\mathcal{P}
.
Then,
\mu^{\prime}:\powerset(\lambda)\to[0,1]
defined by
\mu^{\prime}(A)=\mu(\bigcup_{i\in A}P)
is a real-valued measure on
\lambda
.
Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mu^{\prime}(\lambda)=1
, up to rescaling
\mu^{\prime}
. Thus,
\lambda
is real-valued measurable, as wanted.

(3)
\Rightarrow
(2)
. Assume that
\mu^{\prime}:\powerset(\lambda)\to[0,1]
is a continuous real-valued probabilistic measure on
\lambda
.
Let
Z=\{x_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda\}
be a closed discrete subset of
X
of size
\lambda
as given by Corollary
2.2
.
Define

\mu:\powerset(Z)\to[0,1]
by setting

\mu(M)=\mu^{\prime}(\{\alpha<\lambda\mid x_{\alpha}\in M\}).

Then, it is clear that
(Z,\powerset(Z),\mu)
is a continuous
\lambda
-measure space, and so is
(X,\powerset(X),\mu\uparrow X)
by Lemma
4.20
.
Finally, since
(x_{\alpha})_{\alpha<\lambda}
is a discrete family, we have that
\{x_{\alpha}\mid\alpha<\lambda\}\cap Y
is countable for every second countable
Y\subseteq X
, thus
\mu(Y)=0
for every second countable
Y\subseteq X
, proving that
\mu
cannot be countable-induced.

(2)
\Rightarrow
(1)
. It is enough to notice that for every
\kappa\leq\lambda
there exists a non-
\lambda
-Lindelöf subspace of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
(e.g., the discrete space
X=\{\alpha\conc 0^{(\kappa)}\mid\alpha<\lambda\}
).
∎

Corollary 7.3
.

Assume
\lambda>\mathfrak{c}
. The following are equivalent:

(1)

There is a continuous weak
\lambda
-measure space
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
for some
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
such that
\mu
takes values in
[0,1]
.

(2)

For every non-
\lambda
-Lindelöf space
X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
of weight
\lambda
there is
\mu:\powerset(Y)\to[0,1]
such that
(X,\powerset(X),\mu)
is a continuous weak
\lambda
-measure space.

(3)

\lambda
is measurable.

Proof.

(1)
\Rightarrow
(3)
. Let
(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
be a continuous weak
\lambda
-measure space such that
\mu
takes values in
[0,1]
.
Notice that every second countable subset
Y\subseteq X\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
has size
|Y|\leq\mathfrak{c}<\lambda
, thus
Y
is measurable of measure zero. This shows that

(X,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
cannot be countable-induced.
Hence, the results follows from Proposition
7.2
and the classical result that if
\lambda>\mathfrak{c}
is real-valued measurable, then it is measurable (see, e.g.,
[
16
, Corollary 10.10]
).

(3)
\Rightarrow
(2)
. Follows immediately from Proposition
7.2
and the fact that every measurable cardinal
\lambda
is also real-valued measurable.

(2)
\Rightarrow
(1)
. Obvious.
∎

8.
Final remarks

We conclude the paper with a few final remarks and corollaries that follow easily from the theory developed so far.

First, let us consider the space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.
This space has weight exactly
\lambda^{<\kappa}
. Thus, if
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
, Theorem
1.3
immediately implies that there is no weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure structure on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Corollary 8.1
.

Assume
\lambda^{<\kappa}=\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Then, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

If instead
\lambda^{<\kappa}>\lambda
, then necessarily
\kappa>\omega
, and hence
\lambda^{<\kappa}\geq\mathfrak{c}
. Therefore, by the previous corollary and by Proposition
2.6
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 8.2
.

Let
\delta=\lambda^{<\kappa}
be the weight of
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
, and assume
\delta>\lambda
. Then, there is no continuous weak
\delta^{+}
-measure space
(\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}},\mathfrak{M},\mu)
on
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-7.67737pt{\kappa}\kern 4.74307pt}}_{{\kern-13.88837pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 4.74307pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.98601pt{\kappa}\kern 2.85834pt}}_{{\kern-9.42242pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.85834pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\kappa}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\lambda^{{\kern-4.16934pt{\kappa}\kern 2.04167pt}}_{{\kern-8.60576pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.68175pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.82318pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.01656pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.04167pt}}}
.

Analogous results hold for the generalized Cantor space
\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.60278pt{\lambda}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.09445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.90279pt{\lambda}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.39445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
.
This space has weight
2^{<\lambda}
. Thus, if
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda>\omega
(which in particular implies
\lambda^{<\operatorname{cof}(\lambda)}=\lambda\geq\mathfrak{c}
), then by Theorem
1.3
and Fact
2.7
we obtain the following.

Corollary 8.3
.

Assume
2^{<\lambda}=\lambda>\omega
. Let
Y\subseteq\mathchoice{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-6.95558pt{\lambda}\kern 3.98613pt}}_{{\kern-13.24393pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 3.98613pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-4.60278pt{\lambda}\kern 2.45pt}}_{{\kern-9.09445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 2.45pt}}}{\hphantom{{}^{{{\lambda}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}2^{{\kern-3.90279pt{\lambda}\kern 1.75pt}}_{{\kern-8.39445pt\mathchoice{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[4.74307pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.85834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[2.04167pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}\kern 1.75pt}}}
. Then, there is no continuous weak
\lambda^{+}
-measure space
(Y,\mathfrak{M},\mu)
.

If instead
2^{<\lambda}>\lambda
(and thus
2^{<\lambda}\geq\mathfrak{c}
), by Proposition
2.8
and Theorem
1.3
, we obtain the following.

Corollary 8.4
.

Let
\delta=2^{<\lambda}
be the weight of
